Um, I guess I think about it... a bit. Probably more than I should. I just... I try to push it away because I know it doesn't help anything, but it creeps in.

Um, well, I'm definitely still working. I haven't missed any days or anything, and I think I'm still doing a good job overall. But... I guess I'd be lying if I said it wasn't affecting me at all.

Um, well... I guess I wouldn't say I'm completely reliving it, like a movie or anything dramatic. But, um, sometimes at work, if I'm measuring a railing or if I just hear a ladder scrape against the side of a house, I'll get this... flash, I guess?

I think I'm doing a pretty good job of keeping things contained. That's a good sign, right? And my stomach just completely drops, and my hands get a little shaky for a minute. But I always catch my breath and get right back to work. Um, well, I guess... I mean, I don't really go out of my way to avoid it or anything. If someone brings it up, I'll talk about it. I just try to stay positive, you know? Focus on the fact that he's okay and recovering.

I mean, everyone gets things stuck in their head sometimes, right? I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it. I really hope I'm not wasting your time with all this... is four months a normal amount of time to still be bothered by it?\n\nDoctor: How much of the time do you spend blaming yourself for your husband's fall?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I do think about it a fair amount. I mean, I really try not to dwell on it, because I know accidents happen, right? Like I'm just tense all day long, waiting for the next bad thing to happen. I know I shouldn't beat myself up over it, and I really try to stay positive, but it's just hard to shake that feeling that I should have stopped it from happening. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too sorry for myself.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think about what you could have done to prevent your husband's fall?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess I think about it... a bit. I mean, my work reports are all fine, nobody has said my work is slipping, but... I just can't shake that doubt. I feel like I should be better than that. I hope that's not a silly thing to worry about... do you think it's normal to doubt yourself like that after an accident?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this feeling of guilt over your husband's fall is overwhelming at times?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say overwhelming. I mean, it's there, but I don't want to make it sound like I'm falling apart or anything. I still go to work every day and get things done. It's just... sometimes it gets a little heavy, I guess. If I'm up measuring a railing, or... yeah, especially that sound of a ladder scraping against a wall. \n\nIt's almost like a switch flips, and out of nowhere I'm just seeing him fall all over again, like it's happening right in front of me. And then my stomach just drops and I get those shaky hands. I really try to hide it when it happens, because I know it's just a regular noise and I don't want to overreact or bother anyone at work with it. Am I explaining that okay? I hope that answers what you were asking.\n\nDoctor: How long ago did your husband fall?\n\nPatient: Um, it's been about four months now. It happened when we were repairing the porch at home. I guess I've just been feeling this way since then. I mean, four months isn't that long, right? I'm sure it's normal to still be a little jumpy after something like that. I really hope I'm not wasting your time with all this... is four months a normal amount of time to still be bothered by it?\n\nDoctor: How much of the time do you spend blaming yourself for your husband's fall?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I do think about it a fair amount. I mean, I really try not to dwell on it, because I know accidents happen, right? But... yeah, I guess I've just had this really heavy feeling lately. \n\nIt's just this mix of being scared all the time and... and feeling guilty. Like I'm just tense all day long, waiting for the next bad thing to happen. And I can't help but feel like I should have noticed something was wrong with the ladder, or I should have been holding it better. I know I shouldn't beat myself up over it, and I really try to stay positive, but it's just hard to shake that feeling that I should have stopped it from happening. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too sorry for myself.\n\nDoctor: How often do you think about what you could have done to prevent your husband's fall?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess I think about it... a bit. Probably more than I should. I just... I try to push it away because I know it doesn't help anything, but it creeps in. \n\nI think the hardest part is... well, you know I'm a building inspector, right? So my whole job is noticing things. And I keep thinking that if I missed something at my own house, with my own husband... then maybe I'm just not as careful or competent as I always thought I was. I mean, my work reports are all fine, nobody has said my work is slipping, but... I just can't shake that doubt. I feel like I should be better than that. I hope that's not a silly thing to worry about... do you think it's normal to doubt yourself like that after an accident?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this feeling of guilt over your husband's fall is overwhelming at times?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say overwhelming. I mean, it's there, but I don't want to make it sound like I'm falling apart or anything. I still go to work every day and get things done. It's just... sometimes it gets a little heavy, I guess. But I can usually distract myself if I really try. I'm trying really hard to keep things in perspective. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just keep moving forward?\n\nDoctor: Does this guilt about your husband's fall affect your ability to perform your daily tasks or make decisions?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I'm definitely still working. I haven't missed any days or anything, and I think I'm still doing a good job overall. But... I guess I'd be lying if I said it wasn't affecting me at all. \n\nI've noticed that my inspection reports are taking me a bit longer to finish lately. I just... I catch myself second-guessing things, double-checking everything because I don't want to miss something important again. And, well, I've actually been turning down jobs that involve elevated decks or high porches. I used to do those all the time without even thinking about it, but now I just... I try to pass those on to someone else if I can. \n\nI know I shouldn't let it affect my work, and I'm really trying to get back to normal. It's just a little bump in the road, right? I mean, it's probably pretty common to be a little extra cautious after something like that happens, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or places that you're avoiding because they remind you of your husband's fall?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I mentioned, I have been... sort of avoiding the elevated deck inspections at work. Just the really high ones, you know? I mean, it's not a big deal, my coworkers don't mind taking them. \n\nAnd at home... I guess I try not to go out back where it happened. I mean, I think those are the main things. I'm still doing pretty much everything else the same as always. I still go to the grocery store, I still see my friends, I still drive... all the normal day-to-day stuff. I'm really trying not to let this take over my life, you know? I think I'm doing a pretty good job of keeping things contained. That's a good sign, right? I guess I wouldn't say I'm completely reliving it, like a movie or anything dramatic. I mean, my work reports are all fine, nobody has said my work is slipping, but... I just can't shake that doubt. I feel like I should be better than that. I hope that's not a silly thing to worry about... do you think it's normal to doubt yourself like that after an accident?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this feeling of guilt over your husband's fall is overwhelming at times?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say overwhelming. I mean, it's there, but I don't want to make it sound like I'm falling apart or anything. I still go to work every day and get things done. It's just... sometimes it gets a little heavy, I guess. But I can usually distract myself if I really try. I'm trying really hard to keep things in perspective. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just keep moving forward?\n\nDoctor: Does this guilt about your husband's fall affect your ability to perform your daily tasks or make decisions?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I'm definitely still working. I haven't missed any days or anything, and I think I'm still doing a good job overall. But... I guess I'd be lying if I said it wasn't affecting me at all. \n\nI've noticed that my inspection reports are taking me a bit longer to finish lately. I just... I catch myself second-guessing things, double-checking everything because I don't want to miss something important again. And, well, I've actually been turning down jobs that involve elevated decks or high porches. I used to do those all the time without even thinking about it, but now I just... I try to pass those on to someone else if I can. \n\nI know I shouldn't let it affect my work, and I'm really trying to get back to normal. It's just a little bump in the road, right? I mean, it's probably pretty common to be a little extra cautious after something like that happens, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or places that you're avoiding because they remind you of your husband's fall?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I mentioned, I have been... sort of avoiding the elevated deck inspections at work. Just the really high ones, you know? I mean, it's not a big deal, my coworkers don't mind taking them. \n\nAnd at home... I guess I try not to go out back where it happened. And if my family asks about when I'm going to fix the railing, I usually just change the subject or say I'm too busy right now. I just don't really want to look at it or talk about it yet. But that makes sense, doesn't it? It's pretty normal to want a little space from something like that, right? I'm sure I'll get around to fixing it eventually.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things besides work inspections and your backyard that you've been avoiding because they remind you of your husband's fall?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I think those are the main things. I'm still doing pretty much everything else the same as always. I still go to the grocery store, I still see my friends, I still drive... all the normal day-to-day stuff. I'm really trying not to let this take over my life, you know? I think I'm doing a pretty good job of keeping things contained. That's a good sign, right? That I'm not avoiding everything?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the moment of your husband's fall, like it's happening all over again?\n\nPatient: Um, well... And my stomach just completely drops, and my hands get a little shaky for a minute. But I always catch my breath and get right back to work. I mean, I don't really go out of my way to avoid it or anything. If someone brings it up, I'll talk about it. I just try to stay positive, you know? Focus on the fact that he's okay and recovering. \n\nI don't really see the point in dwelling on the negative feelings or the scary parts of it. That doesn't really help anyone, does it? I just try to keep a good attitude and keep moving forward. I think it's better to just focus on the good things, right? That's the healthy way to handle it, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that avoiding reminders of your husband's fall is interfering with your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I wouldn't say it's interfering, exactly.
